RESULT | PASS | OK | initial_answer=8.149s initial_decision=0.487s rescue_used=False
Mary is able to get cybernetic implants, provided the transaction does not reduce her Essence below her current value of 6.0.

The directly controlling rule is found in passage [2], which states that "Many Shadow Amps, most notably cyberware and bio- ware implants, require the character to trade away part of their metahumanity, and this results in Essence loss." This establishes that acquiring cyberware is a permitted action that consumes Essence.

Passage [4] provides the specific limitation: a character cannot acquire an implant if the "Essence cost would reduce the character to an Essence level" (implied: below their current rating). Since Mary's current Essence is 6.0, she may purchase any cyberware with an Essence cost of 5 or less. She cannot purchase any item with a cost of 6 or higher, as that would drop her Essence to 5.0 or lower.

No other passages in the supplied text prohibit Awakened characters or Dwarves from using cyberware, nor do they impose additional Essence caps beyond the general rule of not dropping below the current rating. Passages [1], [3], [5], [6], [7], [8] discuss specific examples, restrictions on corporations, or qualities but do not alter the fundamental rule regarding Essence loss for cyberware.
FINAL DECISION-ONLY RAW: YES

RESULT | PASS | OK | initial_answer=8.404s initial_decision=0.442s rescue_used=False
Mary can attempt to fly the helicopter using **Agility** as an untrained alternative.

The sheet lists **Piloting (Other)** as a required skill for vehicles that move on something besides ground (helicopters fall under this category per [5]), but Mary does not have this skill. However, rule [1] explicitly states that if a character is untrained in the appropriate Piloting Skill, they may use **Agility** instead. Since Mary has Agility 5, she is permitted to make the test using that attribute.

**Directly controlling rules:**
*   **Skill Requirement:** Helicopters require **Piloting (Other)** because they move on something besides ground ([5]).
*   **Untrained Alternative:** If the specific Piloting skill is not possessed, the character uses **Agility** ([1]).
*   **Absence of Prohibition:** The absence of Piloting (Other) on the sheet is not a prohibition because the rules provide a valid untrained alternative ([1]).
FINAL DECISION-ONLY RAW: YES

RESULT | PASS | OK | initial_answer=7.098s initial_decision=0.599s rescue_used=False
Mary can attempt to fly the helicopter using **Agility** as an untrained alternative.

The sheet lists **Piloting (Other)** as a required skill for vehicles that move on something besides ground (helicopters fall under this category per [5]), but Mary does not have this skill. However, rule [1] explicitly states that if a character is untrained in the appropriate Piloting Skill, they may use **Agility** instead ("or just Agility, if untrained"). Since Mary has Agility 5, she meets the attribute requirement for this untrained attempt.

**Directly controlling rules:**
*   **Skill Scope:** Helicopters require **Piloting (Other)** because they move on something besides ground ([5]).
*   **Untrained Alternative:** If a character lacks the specific Piloting skill, they may use **Agility** to perform the action ([1]).
*   **Absence of Prohibition:** The absence of a skill on the sheet is not a prohibition unless a rule explicitly forbids the action without it; rule [1] provides the explicit default allowing the use of Agility ([1]).

Mary can attempt to fly the helicopter using **Agility** as an untrained alternative.

The sheet lists **Piloting (Other)** as a required skill for vehicles that move on something besides ground (helicopters fall under this category per [5]), but Mary does not have this skill. However, rule [1] explicitly states that if a character is untrained in the appropriate Piloting Skill, they may use **Agility** instead ("or just Agility, if untrained"). Since Mary has Agility 5, she meets the attribute requirement for this untrained attempt.

**Directly controlling rules:**
*   **Skill Scope:** Helicopters require **Piloting (Other)** because they move on something besides ground ([5]).
*   **Untrained Alternative:** If a character lacks the specific Piloting skill, they may use **Agility** to perform the action ([1]).
*   **Absence of Prohibition:** The absence of a skill on the sheet is not a prohibition unless a rule explicitly forbids the action without it; rule [1] provides the explicit default allowing the use of Agility ([1]).

While Mary possesses the Heavy Weapons skill (Rating 3) and has grenades as a weapon, the literal definition in the provided text restricts the Heavy Weapons skill to specific mounted or launcher-based delivery systems (grenade launchers), not manual throwing. Therefore, based strictly on the supplied passages, the Heavy Weapons skill does not apply to throwing a grenade by hand; that action falls under the "Projectile Weapons" category.

Based on the supplied character sheet and rules passages, the analysis is as follows:

**Directly Controlling Rule:**
Passage [2] explicitly defines the scope of the **Heavy Weapons** skill. It lists "Machine guns, assault cannons, missile launchers, and **grenade launchers**." It does not list "fragmentation grenades" or the act of throwing them by hand.

**Application to Character:**
Mary possesses the **Heavy Weapons** skill (Rating 3) and carries **fragmentation grenades**. However, the skill definition in [2] restricts its application to specific delivery systems (launchers), not the handheld throwing of the munition itself.

**Explicit Defaults and Untrained Alternatives:**
The character sheet lists a **Knowledge / Custom Skill: Blowing things up** (Rating 0). While this suggests the character has knowledge of explosives, the rating is 0, meaning it provides no mechanical bonus to rolls. There is no rule passage provided that states throwing grenades by hand falls under Heavy Weapons, nor is there a rule stating that a character without a specific "Throwing" skill cannot throw grenades (absence is not a prohibition). However, since the specific skill she has (Heavy Weapons) is defined by [2] to cover launchers, it does not literally apply to the act of throwing a grenade by hand.

**Conclusion on Applicability:**
The **Heavy Weapons** skill does not apply to throwing a grenade by hand because the skill's definition in [2] is limited to "grenade launchers," excluding the manual throwing of grenades. Mary would likely rely on her untrained ability or the "Blowing things up" knowledge (which offers no rating bonus) for this action, as no rule explicitly grants Heavy Weapons coverage for hand-thrown grenades.

The character sheet lists the Quality as "Allergy (Choose)" with the custom entry "Orcs" and the effect "uncontrolable sneezing." It does not explicitly state the penalty value on the sheet itself.

The directly controlling rule is found in **[2]**, which defines the "Allergy (Specify)" quality: "When affected by allergy (GM deci- sion), –4 to all dice rolls."

Applying this rule literally to Mary's "Allergy (Choose)" quality:
1.  The quality type matches "Allergy."
2.  The trigger is "When affected by allergy."
3.  The effect is explicitly "–4 to all dice rolls."

The rule does not limit the penalty to specific skill types (e.g., social rolls) or specific contexts (e.g., involving orcs). The phrase "all dice rolls" is unambiguous. Therefore, if the GM decides Mary is affected by her Orc allergy, she suffers a -4 penalty to every dice roll she makes, regardless of the skill or situation.

**Conclusion:** Yes, the penalty applies to every dice roll.

The character sheet lists the Quality as "Allergy (Choose)" with the custom entry "Orcs" and the effect "uncontrolable sneezing."

The directly controlling rule is found in passage [2], which defines the **Allergy (Specify)** quality: "When affected by allergy (GM deci- sion), –4 to all dice rolls."

Applying this rule literally:
1.  The quality on the sheet is an Allergy.
2.  The rule for Allergy (Specify) imposes a **–4 penalty to all dice rolls** when affected.
3.  The rule does not limit this penalty to specific skill types (e.g., social rolls); it applies to "all dice rolls."

Therefore, if Mary's Orc allergy is triggered, the –4 penalty applies to every dice roll she makes while affected, not just social rolls.

RESULT | PASS | OK | initial_answer=9.844s initial_decision=0.509s rescue_used=False
The directly controlling rule is found in the Quality assignment section of the Core Rulebook.

**Rule Application:**
Passage [6] explicitly states that positive qualities are assigned to "make the two positive qualities into +2 dice bonuses for a relevant skill." It does not specify that these bonuses are restricted to *making* a skill test, nor does it limit the application to *offensive* use of the skill.

Passages [1], [5], and others describe the specific effect of the **Guts** quality: "May reroll 2 dice when resisting fear or intimidation."

**Conclusion:**
Since Mary has the **Guts** quality listed on her sheet, and the rule for assigning positive qualities grants them as "+2 dice bonuses for a relevant skill," the Guts quality functions as a +2 dice bonus to her Intimidation skill (which she possesses at Rating 4). The specific text of the Guts quality ("reroll 2 dice when resisting fear or intimidation") describes the mechanical outcome of that bonus in the context of resisting intimidation. The fact that Guts is a Quality rather than a Skill does not prevent it from applying to the Intimidation skill, as the assignment rule in [6] applies qualities to skills generally. Therefore, the Guts quality allows her to reroll two dice when resisting an Intimidation attempt.
FINAL DECISION-ONLY RAW: YES

The directly controlling rule is found in the Quality assignment section, which states that positive qualities are translated into "+2 dice bonuses for a relevant skill" [6]. The specific effect of the Guts quality is defined as "May reroll 2 dice when resisting fear or intimidation" [1], [5].

Applying these rules literally:
1.  **Relevance:** Intimidation is a Skill listed on Mary's sheet [Selected character context]. Therefore, Guts is a "relevant skill" for the purpose of the Quality assignment rule [6].
2.  **Application:** The rule does not require the Quality to be a Skill itself; it requires the Quality to apply to a relevant Skill. Since Intimidation is a Skill, the Guts Quality applies to it.
3.  **Effect:** The specific text of Guts allows rerolling two dice when resisting intimidation [1], [5].

Conclusion: Yes, Mary can reroll two dice. The rule [6] explicitly converts the positive Quality into a bonus for a relevant Skill (Intimidation), and the specific text of Guts [1], [5] grants the reroll effect for resisting intimidation. The fact that Guts is a Quality rather than a Skill does not prevent this, as the system is designed to translate Qualities into bonuses for Skills.

RESULT | PASS | OK | initial_answer=8.996s initial_decision=0.471s rescue_used=False
Based on the supplied character sheet and rules passages, here is the analysis:

**Directly Controlling Rules:**
*   **Action Limit:** Passage [1] states that "each player can take one combat-related action" per Narration.
*   **Spell Classification:** Passage [5] explicitly states that "Casting a spell counts as an attack action for your Narration."
*   **Weapon Usage Restriction:** Passage [6] states that a player cannot use "individual weapons during that same Narration" if they are performing a specific combat action (context implies this restriction applies when the action slot is consumed by another combat method, such as casting a spell which counts as an attack action).

**Application to Mary:**
1.  Mary casts **Implode**, which is a **Spell** (Sheet: Shadow Amp #1).
2.  According to [5], casting this spell counts as an **attack action**.
3.  According to [1], Mary is limited to **one** combat-related action per Narration.
4.  Firing her **SMG** is a separate combat action (using an individual weapon).
5.  Because the spell already consumes her single allowed combat action for that Narration, she cannot also fire her SMG in the same Narration.

**Conclusion:**
No, she cannot fire her SMG in the same Narration because casting the spell consumes her single allowed combat action.
FINAL DECISION-ONLY RAW: NO

Based on the supplied character sheet and rules passages, here is the analysis:

**Directly Controlling Rules:**
*   **Action Limit:** Passage [1] states that "each player can take one combat-related action" per Narration.
*   **Spell Classification:** Passage [5] explicitly states that "Casting a spell counts as an attack action for your Narration."
*   **Weapon Usage Restriction:** Passage [6] states that a player cannot use "individual weapons during that same Narration" if they are performing a specific combat action (context implies this restriction applies when the action slot is consumed by another combat method, such as casting a spell which counts as an attack action).

**Application to Mary:**
1.  Mary casts **Implode**, which is a **Spell** (Sheet: Shadow Amp #1).
2.  According to [5], casting this spell counts as an **attack action**.
3.  According to [1], Mary is limited to **one** combat-related action per Narration.
4.  Firing her **SMG** is a separate combat action (using an individual weapon).
5.  Because the spell already consumes her single allowed combat action for that Narration, she cannot also fire her SMG in the same Narration.

**Conclusion:**
No, she cannot fire her SMG in the same Narration because casting the spell consumes her single allowed combat action.

*   **Passage [2]** defines the condition for earning a Target Token: "Any time a player repeats an action more than once in consecutive Narrations—firing the same gun without moving, casting the same spell, etc.—"

**Application to Mary:**
1.  **Action Classification:** Mary casting the Shadow Amp *Implode* (a spell) counts as an **attack action** for her Narration per Passage [5].
